                                                               2015 Mathematical Contest in Modeling®
                                                                    Press Release—April 6, 2015

COMAP is pleased to announce the results of the 31st annual Mathematical
Contest in Modeling (MCM). This year, 7636 teams representing                                        The B problem was authored by Professor William Fox, entitled “Searching
institutions from seventeen countries participated in the contest. Ten teams                         for a lost plane”.

WINNERS:

Central South University, China (Ben Fusaro Award)                                                    plane feared to have crashed in open water such as the Atlantic, Pacific,
Chongqing University, China (SIAM Award)                                                              Indian, Southern, or Arctic Ocean while flying from Point A to Point B.
Colorado College, Colorado Springs, CO (INFORMS Award)                                                Assume that there are no signals from the downed plane. Your model
Northwestern Polytechnical University, China                                                          should recognize that there are many different types of planes for which

This year’s contest ran from Thursday, February 5 to Monday, February 9,                             judges. All 7636 of the competing teams are to be congratulated for their
2015. During that time, teams of up to three undergraduate or high school                            excellent work and enthusiasm for mathematical modeling and
students researched, modeled, and submitted a solution to one of two                                 interdisciplinary problem solving.
modeling problems. The 2015 MCM was primarily an online contest.
Teams registered, obtained contest materials, and downloaded the problem
and data at the prescribed time through COMAP’s MCM Website.                                                                         2015 MCM Statistics

This year the two MCM problems represented significant challenges. The                                     7636 teams participated
author of the A problem is Professor William Fox. The problem was                                          5356 Problem A (70%)
“Eradicating Ebola”.
                                                                                                           2280 Problem B (30%)
                                                                                                           418 US Teams (5%)
The world medical association has announced that their new medication
                                                                                                           7218 Foreign Teams (95%) from Australia, Canada, China,
could stop Ebola and cure patients whose disease is not advanced. Build
a realistic, sensible, and useful model that considers not only the spread
                                                                                                            Finland, Hong Kong SAR, India, Indonesia, Mexico,
of the disease, the quantity of the medicine needed, possible feasible                                      Netherlands, Scotland, Singapore, South Korea, Spain,
delivery systems, locations of delivery, speed of manufacturing of the                                      Sweden, Taiwan and United Kingdom
vaccine or drug, but also any other critical factors your team considers                                   10 Outstanding Winners (1%)
necessary as part of the model to optimize the eradication of Ebola, or at                                 12 Finalist Winners (1%)
least its current strain. In addition to your modeling approach for the                                    641 Meritorious Winners (9%)
contest, prepare a 1-2 page non-technical letter for the world medical
                                                                                                           2266 Honorable Mentions (31%)
association to use in their announcement.
                                                                                                           4685 Successful Participants (57%)
                                                                                                           22 Unsuccessful Participants (1%)



    To obtain additional information about the MCM and to obtain a complete listing of all team designations, please visit the MCM Website at: www.mcmcontest.com, or contact COMAP at:
    mcm@comap.com.

    Major funding for the MCM is provided by COMAP. Additional support is provided by the Institute for Operations Research and the Management Sciences (INFORMS) and Two Sigma
    Investments. COMAP's Mathematical Contest in Modeling and Interdisciplinary Contest in Modeling are unique among modeling competitions in that they are the only international contests
    in which students work in teams to find a solution. Centering its educational philosophy on mathematical modeling, COMAP uses mathematical tools to explore real-world problems. It serves
    the educational community as well as the world of work by preparing students to become better informed—and prepared—citizens, consumers, and workers.




    Contest Director                                                                                     Founding Director
       William P. Fox, Naval Postgraduate School, Monterey, CA                                               Ben Fusaro, Florida State University

    Executive Director                                                                                   Associate Director
        Solomon A. Garfunkel, COMAP, Inc., Bedford, MA                                                       Pat Driscoll, United States Military Academy, NY
